Math Research Digest

Welcome to this week's issue of Grounded, covering an exceptionally rich fortnight for mathematics. Two themes stand out immediately. First, transcendence and irrationality are having a moment in unexpected places: one paper proves that Hilbert-Kunz multiplicity — a prime-characteristic ring invariant studied for decades under the assumption it was at least algebraic — can be genuinely transcendental, while another reframes the Riemann Hypothesis itself as a statement about the polynomial growth of moments of a probabilistic object built from the Möbius function. Second, geometry in the limit is everywhere: we see the first proof that area-minimizing submanifolds are *not* generically smooth except in the classical low-codimension cases (settling a conjecture of White), a near-complete resolution of the lattice covering density problem that drops the best bound from quadratic to nearly linear in dimension, and a striking result showing that fractal (Cantor set) spectra force orthogonal polynomial zero statistics into genuinely new universality classes that cycle periodically rather than converging. Elsewhere, the week delivers a steady stream of long-standing conjectures dispatched: the Polycirculant Conjecture falls to an explicit infinite family of counterexamples, phase retrieval in ℂ⁴ is pinned at exactly eleven measurements via algebraic topology, and the

Number Theory

21

$\mathbb Q\setminus\mathbb Z$ is diophantine over $\mathbb Q$ with $7$ unknowns

Zhi-Wei Sun

A set is 'diophantine over ℚ' if membership can be detected by whether a rational polynomial equation has rational solutions. This paper shows the non-integers ℚ\ℤ are diophantine over ℚ using just 7 unknowns, improving the previous record of 10. Minimizing unknowns matters because it edges closer to resolving Hilbert's Tenth Problem over ℚ—one of the biggest open questions in logic and number theory.

math.NT math.LO
Original abstract

In 2016 J. Koenigsmann proved that $\mathbb Q\setminus\mathbb Z$ is diophantine over $\mathbb Q$, i.e., there is a polynomial $P(t,x_1,\ldots,x_{n})\in\mathbb Z[t,x_1,\ldots,x_{n}]$ such that for any rational number $t$ we have $$t\not\in\mathbb Z\iff \exists x_1,\ldots,x_{n}\in\mathbb Q\,[P(t,x_1,\ldots,x_{n})=0].$$ In this paper we show that we may take $n=7$ which improves the previous record $n=10$ obtained by Daans in 2024. (Actually we even extend this to any global field.) This, together with a previous result of Z.-W. Sun, implies that there is no algorithm to decide for any $F(x_1,\ldots,x_{16})\in\mathbb Z[x_1,\ldots,x_{16}]$ whether $$\forall x_1,\ldots,x_9\in\mathbb Q\exists y_1,\ldots,y_{7}\in\mathbb Q\,[F(x_1,\ldots,x_9,y_1,\ldots,y_{7})=0].$$

Schwartz spaces on L-monoids: non-Archimedean

Chun-Hsien Hsu, HaoYun Yao

The Braverman-Kazhdan-Ngô program seeks to construct generalized Schwartz spaces and Fourier transforms that encode the Langlands functoriality conjectures (deep links between number theory and representation theory). This paper completes that program over non-Archimedean local fields (like p-adic numbers) under mild, natural assumptions, and does so unconditionally for general linear groups—a significant step toward making the analytic side of the Langlands program fully rigorous.

math.NT math.RT
Original abstract

We complete the Braverman-Kazhdan-Ngô program over non-Archimedean local fields assuming local Langlands conjecture for tempered representations and a natural assumption on the $γ$-factors of non-supercuspidal discrete series. In particular, the program is complete unconditionally for general linear groups.

Random linear configurations in dense sets and primes

Lasse Grimmelt, Joni Teräväinen

How dense must a set of integers be to guarantee it contains arithmetic-like patterns? This paper shows that sets of density roughly 1/(log N)^c in {1,…,N}—called polylogarithmically dense—contain configurations x+b₁m,…,x+bₖm for almost all coefficient vectors (b₁,…,bₖ). The same holds within the primes. Key innovations include a new quantitative von Neumann theorem and norm-lowering techniques that reduce the problem to Fourier-analytic estimates.

math.NT math.CO
Original abstract

We prove that every polylogarithmically dense subset of $[N]$ contains a nontrivial configuration $x+b_1m,\ldots,x+b_km$ for almost all choices of the coefficient vector $(b_1,\ldots, b_k)$ in a wide range of scales. We prove the same statement for polylogarithmically relatively dense subsets of the primes, in a shorter range of scales. The main ingredients are a new quantitative generalised von Neumann theorem, degree lowering to the $U^{1+}$ norm, and densification arguments that transfer the result to the primes.

Schur Eisenstein series and Schur MacMahon series

Henrik Bachmann, Jinbo Yu

Quasimodular forms are functions with structured transformation properties under modular symmetries, generalizing classical modular forms. This paper builds two new families of them by plugging Schur functions — symmetric polynomials indexed by partitions — into classical Eisenstein and MacMahon series. The connection between the two families runs through a Hopf algebra structure, and the Schur Eisenstein series for small partitions are shown to actually span all quasimodular forms.

math.NT math.CO math.QA
Original abstract

We introduce and study two partition-indexed families of quasimodular forms obtained from Schur functions: Schur Eisenstein series and Schur MacMahon series. An explicit transition between them can be interpreted as a convolution in a Faà di Bruno Hopf algebra of symmetric functions. We discuss the classical sl2-action and prove that Schur Eisenstein series for partitions with parts of size at most 3 give a basis for quasimodular forms. Further, we conjecture that the Schur MacMahon series span all quasimodular forms with integral coefficients.

On the second moment and non-vanishing of central values of Hecke $L$-functions of $r$-th order characters

Adrian Diaconu, Bogdan Ion, Vicenţiu Paşol et al.

Hecke L-functions generalize the Riemann zeta function to number fields, and their central values encode deep arithmetic information. This paper establishes precise asymptotic formulas for averaged products of these L-functions for higher-order characters, then uses them to show that a positive proportion of these central values are nonzero. Nonvanishing results matter because a zero central value often signals special arithmetic structure, so ruling it out generically has significant consequences for number theory.

math.NT
Original abstract

In this paper, we establish asymptotic formulas for the first and second twisted moments of $r$-th order Hecke $L$-functions over global fields that contain the $2r$-th roots of unity, for $r\ge 3$. We focus primarily on algebraic number fields. As a consequence, we establish a positive proportion of non-vanishing central values for these $L$-functions, specifically for families of both square-free and $r$-th power-free ideals. Our approach is based on the machinery of multiple Dirichlet series.

The exceptional set of the Goldbach problem

Lasse Grimmelt, Gautami Bhowmik

Goldbach's conjecture says every even integer greater than two is a sum of two primes; the 'exceptional set' counts even integers up to N that fail this. This survey and new-results paper traces progress on bounding that exceptional set, from Hardy-Littlewood's circle method through Montgomery-Vaughan and Pintz, and delivers an explicit formula for the main contributing terms. The new result also rules out exceptions near certain arithmetic progressions.

math.NT
Original abstract

We study the estimates for the number of exceptions to the representation of integers as the sum of at most two prime numbers. Most of this article is a survey that gives an overview of existing results. We begin with the legendary Hardy-Littlewood circle method and show how it paved the way to a power saving by Montgomery-Vaughan in 1975 and Pintz in 2018. We conclude with a new result that is a fully explicit formula for the major arcs. Another new observation is the non-existence of exceptional zeros under a sparse version of the Hardy-Littlewood conjecture. The survey part of this article aims to be accessible to an audience that has not encountered these techniques before.

Explicit mock Heegner points and BSD formula on certain Mordell curves

Sumita Gunri, Somnath Jha, Dipramit Majumdar

The Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer (BSD) conjecture predicts a deep connection between an elliptic curve's rational points and an associated L-function. For Mordell curves of the form X³+Y³=2a, the authors explicitly construct Heegner points — special rational points built from arithmetic of imaginary quadratic fields — and verify the BSD formula holds up to a 2-adic unit for two infinite families of such curves, providing rare explicit evidence for BSD in previously inaccessible cases.

math.NT
Original abstract

For a natural number $a$, let $E_{2a}$ be the Mordell elliptic curve $X^3 +Y^3=2a$. We give an explicit construction of (mock) Heegner point on the Mordell curve $E_{2p}$ for a prime $p\equiv 4 \mod 9$ and $E_{2p^2}$ for a prime $p\equiv 7 \mod 9$, under the assumption that $2$ is not a cube modulo $p$. We also verify the explicit Gross-Zagier formula for these curves and go on to show that the BSD formula holds for these curves up to a $2$-adic unit. Using a result of Burungale-Flach, we show that the full BSD formula holds for the rank zero curve $E_{2p}$ for $p\equiv 7 \mod 9$ and $E_{2p^2}$ for $p\equiv 4 \mod 9$, whenever $2$ is not a cube modulo $p$.

Intersection Cohomology of Igusa Stacks

Ana Caraiani, Linus Hamann, Mingjia Zhang

Shimura varieties are geometric spaces central to number theory and the Langlands program; their intersection cohomology (a refined homology theory for singular spaces) encodes deep arithmetic information. This paper uses Igusa stacks and the Fargues-Scholze framework to construct a sheaf on a geometric object called the Fargues-Fontaine curve that recovers this cohomology, proving it is Verdier self-dual and perverse. This advances the geometric Langlands correspondence in the p-adic setting.

math.NT math.AG math.RT
Original abstract

We study the intersection cohomology of minimally compactified Shimura varieties of PEL type AC using Igusa stacks and the work of Fargues-Scholze. More precisely, we construct a sheaf on the moduli stack of $G$-bundles on the Fargues-Fontaine curve, which recovers this intersection cohomology after applying a Hecke operator in the sense of geometric Langlands. We show that this sheaf has several desirable properties; for example, it is Verdier self-dual and perverse. This leads to several applications to intersection cohomology, including a version of the Mantovan product formula, as well as torsion-vanishing and Eichler-Shimura relations. Along the way, we investigate the interaction between Baily-Borel and Newton stratifications on minimally compactified Igusa stacks, and we study perverse t-structures on stratified v-stacks.

Beyond endoscopy for the symmetric square representation: The simple trace formula case

Yuhao Cheng

Langlands' 'Beyond Endoscopy' program is a broad strategy for proving functoriality—deep relationships between automorphic forms on different groups—using the trace formula. This paper extends the approach to the symmetric square representation with ramification, deriving an asymptotic formula that advances the program beyond previously studied unramified settings.

math.NT math.RT
Original abstract

At the beginning of this century, Langlands introduced a strategy known as \emph{Beyond Endoscopy} to attack the principle of functoriality. Altuğ studied $\mathsf{GL}_2$ over $\mathbb Q$ in the unramified setting for the standard representation. We consider the case with ramification at $S=\{\infty,q_1,\dots,q_r\}$ with $2\in S$ and derive an asymptotic formula for the symmetric square representation adding some additional conditions on the test function so that the trace formula is simple. The limit is nonzero in general and we may detect the dihedral forms by using such limit form of the trace formula which is similar to Venkatesh's thesis. The proof involves a second Poisson summation, computation of the transformed Kloosterman sum and the corresponding series, and giving an asymptotic formula for the main term by residue analysis and using technical analysis to deal with the error term.

On the Vanishing of the Brauer-Manin Obstruction for Normic Bundles

Mridul Biswas, Divyasree C-Ramachandran, Biswanath Samanta

The Brauer-Manin obstruction is an algebraic tool that can explain why a variety has points over every local field but no global rational point. For a class of varieties called normic bundles, this paper shows the obstruction vanishes after passing to a field extension of appropriately divisible degree, meaning rational points become unobstructed. This clarifies how the obstruction behaves under base change and provides explicit degree conditions for specific cases.

math.NT
Original abstract

We study the behaviour of the Brauer--Manin obstruction to the existence of rational points under finite field extensions. For $(p, mp)$-normic bundles over number fields, we prove that the Brauer--Manin obstruction vanishes after base change to finite extensions whose degrees satisfy suitable $p$-divisibility conditions depending on $m$. We further show that, for $(p,2p)$-normic bundles with $p=2$ or $3$, it is enough to assume that the extension degree is divisible by $p$. We also prove that the divisibility hypothesis is, in general optimal, by constructing a conic bundle for which the Brauer--Manin obstruction persists over a quadratic extension.

Diagonal cycles on Shtukas and the adjoint $L$-function

Zeyu Wang

Gross–Zagier formulas link geometric intersection numbers on modular curves to derivatives of L-functions, connecting number theory and geometry. This paper establishes such a formula over function fields for reductive groups of arbitrary type, relating self-intersection numbers of diagonal cycles on shtuka moduli spaces to higher derivatives of adjoint L-functions. The result suggests a conjectural parallel picture for Shimura varieties, pointing toward a broad generalization of classical Gross–Zagier theory.

math.NT math.AG math.RT
Original abstract

We study diagonal cycles on moduli spaces of shtukas for groups of the form $H\times H$, where $H$ is a split almost simple reductive group, allowing arbitrary modification types. We relate their self-intersection numbers, with insertions of determinant line bundles, to higher derivatives of adjoint $L$-functions. This gives a general Gross--Zagier-type identity over function fields for groups of arbitrary type, and suggests a parallel conjectural picture for arithmetic intersections on Shimura varieties.

The Explicit Hypergeometric Modularity Method III

Brian Grove, Ling Long, Esme Rosen et al.

Hypergeometric Galois representations are arithmetic objects built from functions generalizing classical hypergeometric series; proving them modular means linking them to modular forms, a deep number-theoretic connection. This paper refines the Explicit Hypergeometric Modularity Method and applies it to length-4 hypergeometric data, confirming modularity for nine Calabi-Yau threefolds whose associated modular forms had only been conjectured. It also extends the method beyond data defined over the rationals.

math.NT
Original abstract

We refine the Explicit Hypergeometric Modularity Method (EHMM) and develop a variant that applies to a broader class of hypergeometric data. As an application, we establish the modularity of hypergeometric Galois representations arising from length-$4$ data that are not necessarily defined over $\mathbb{Q}$. We also use this method to give explicit constructions of nine modular forms associated with hypergeometric rigid Calabi-Yau threefolds conjectured to be modular by Rodriguez-Villegas. The modularity of these threefolds was first proved by Long-Tu-Yui-Zudilin using a different approach based on the Faltings-Serre method. Moreover, the explicit nature of the method makes it well suited to the computation of special $L$-values of the associated modular forms.

Twisted automorphic descent to odd GSpin groups and applications

Pan Yan

Automorphic descent is a technique that constructs automorphic representations (analytic objects encoding number-theoretic symmetries) on smaller groups from larger ones. This paper establishes such a descent from GL_{2n} to odd GSpin groups, generalizing prior work, and uses it to give a new explicit construction of the Jacquet-Langlands correspondence—a fundamental bridge between automorphic forms on GL_2 and on division algebras over number fields.

math.NT math.RT
Original abstract

We establish twisted automorphic descent from $(\mathrm{GL}_{2n}, \mathrm{GSpin}_2^δ)$, where $\mathrm{GSpin}_2^δ$ is anisotropic, to the odd $\mathrm{GSpin}$ group $\mathrm{GSpin}^{δ,α}_{2n+1}$, which may be split or non-split, generalizing a construction of Jiang--Liu--Xu--Zhang. As an application, we give a new explicit construction of the global Jacquet--Langlands correspondence between $\mathrm{GL}_2$ and $D^\times$, where $D$ is a quaternion division algebra over a number field. As another application, we prove a new case of the global Gan--Gross--Prasad conjecture for $(\mathrm{GSpin}_{2n+1}, \mathrm{GSpin}^δ_2)$.

On $C^k$-functions mapping $\mathbb{Q}$ into itself and Mahler's problem on Liouville numbers

Jean Lelis, Carlos Gustavo Moreira, Elaine Silva

Liouville numbers are transcendental reals approximable by rationals far better than algebraic numbers, and Mahler asked whether smooth transcendental functions can map Liouville numbers to Liouville numbers. This paper constructs, for any smoothness class C^k, an uncountable set of such functions mapping rationals to rationals while preserving the Liouville property, giving a positive and precise answer to Mahler's question in the finite-smoothness setting.

math.NT
Original abstract

Liouville numbers form a classical class of transcendental real numbers characterized by exceptionally strong rational approximations. A theorem of Maillet shows that non-constant rational functions with rational coefficients preserve the Liouville property, motivating a question of Mahler on whether analogous phenomena hold for transcendental functions. In this paper, we address this problem for real functions of finite smoothness. For any $\varepsilon>0$, we construct an uncountable set of $C^k$-functions on $\mathbb{R}$, dense with respect to the topology of uniform convergence on compact sets, mapping $\mathbb{Q}$ into itself and satisfying $\operatorname{den}(f(p/q)) \le q^{2k+\varepsilon}$, and deduce that such functions preserve Liouville numbers. In contrast, we prove a rigidity result about a $C^{2k+1}$-function mapping $\mathbb{Q}$ into itself and satisfying $\operatorname{den}(f(p/q)) \ll q^k$.

Bilinear forms with Kloosterman sums via quadratic characters

Valentin Blomer, Alexandru Pascadi

Kloosterman sums are exponential sums central to analytic number theory, and bounding their bilinear forms unlocks results about L-functions and prime distribution. This paper achieves new savings of c^{−1/32} over the trivial bound in the hardest 'critical range,' valid for all moduli—improving on all prior work even for primes. The key innovation is an unexpected connection to quadratic character sums, with applications to moments of twisted L-functions and exceptional Maass forms.

math.NT
Original abstract

We prove new bounds for bilinear forms with Kloosterman sums, valid for all moduli c. In the critical range where the summation length is the square root of the modulus, the saving over the trivial bound is c^{-1/32}, improving on all previous approaches even for prime moduli. This is based on a new connection to quadratic character sums. Applications to moments of twisted L-functions and to the large sieve for exceptional Maass forms are given.

Signs of Square-Free Fourier Coefficients of Half-Integral weight cusp forms and the Congruent Number Problem

Wei Tao, Guo Xuejun

Half-integral weight modular forms have Fourier coefficients indexed by integers, and a basic question is: how evenly do those coefficients split between positive and negative values? This paper proves that for a broad class of such forms, both positive and negative square-free coefficients up to X number at least ~X^{4/7}, establishing genuine sign oscillation. As an application, this gives new information about which integers are congruent numbers — areas of right triangles with rational sides — via the Shimura correspondence linking weight-3/2 forms to elliptic curves.

math.NT
Original abstract

We study the sign distribution of square-free Fourier coefficients of half-integral weight cusp forms. We prove that, for half-integral weight cusp forms \(f\) satisfying the eigenform conditions and having a nonzero cuspidal Shimura lift, the numbers of positive and negative square-free Fourier coefficients up to \(X\) are both \(\gg_{f,\varepsilon}X^{4/7-\varepsilon}\). As an application to the congruent-number problem, we consider the weight \(3/2\) cusp form whose Shimura lift is the weight \(2\) newform attached to $ E:y^2=x^3-x$. We prove that each each sign occurs among its square-free Fourier coefficients at \(\gg_{\varepsilon}X^{4/7-\varepsilon}\) odd square-free integers \(t\leq X\). In particular, the square-free Fourier coefficients change sign infinitely often.

Decision trees, Frobenius traces, and Weierstrass coefficients of elliptic curves

Barinder S. Banwait, Xiaoyu Huang, Kyu-Hwan Lee et al.

Elliptic curves over the rationals are described partly by Weierstrass coefficients, and Frobenius traces are arithmetic fingerprints of the curve at each prime. This paper proves that the first three reduced minimal Weierstrass coefficients can be exactly recovered from just two Frobenius traces (at primes 2 and 3), plus conductor parity—using decision tree models to discover the pattern, then proving it rigorously. The result gives a surprisingly compact encoding of key curve data.

math.NT cs.LG
Original abstract

We investigate the extent to which the reduced minimal Weierstrass coefficients of an elliptic curve over $\mathbb{Q}$ may be computed from it's Frobenius traces. Decision tree models reveal that the first two reduced minimal Weierstrass coefficients can be recovered with perfect accuracy from the Frobenius traces at the primes $2$ and $3$, and the third by supplementing these two traces with the conductor parity. We subsequently prove explicit formulae for these coefficients using the Frobenius traces and conductor parity. These formulae appear to be new. In particular, we deduce that the first three reduced minimal Weierstrass coefficients of an elliptic curve are determined by its isogeny class.

Slowly Divergent Trajectories for Weighted Singular Vectors in R^2

Bohan Yang

How slowly can a 'singular vector' drift toward irrationality? Singular vectors are real vectors that are unusually well-approximated by rationals in a weighted sense. This paper shows that even vectors whose approximation quality degrades as slowly as possible—just barely qualifying as singular—still form a set of the same Hausdorff dimension (a measure of fractal size) as all singular vectors. This confirms that near-singular behavior is geometrically as rich as full singularity.

math.NT math.DS
Original abstract

Let $w=(w_1,w_2)$ satisfy $w_1>w_2>0$ and $w_1+w_2=1$. For every function $f:[0,\infty)\to(0,\infty)$ with $f(t)\to0$, we prove that the set of $w$-singular vectors whose weighted shortest-vector function satisfies $W_x(t)\ge\log f(t)$ for all sufficiently large $t$ has Hausdorff dimension $s_w=2-(1+w_1)^{-1}$, equal to the full Hausdorff dimension of $\operatorname{Sing}_w(2)$. For every $0<ν<1$ and $μ>0$, a separate power schedule gives Hausdorff dimension $s_w$ for the weighted uniform approximation set with rate $Q^{-1}\exp\bigl(-μ(\log Q)^ν\bigr)$, whereas the lower-envelope theorem gives the same dimension for its complement in $\operatorname{Sing}_w(2)$. We give a direct proof of the lower-envelope theorem by adapting the self-affine construction of Liao--Shi--Solan--Tamam to a variable sequence of return times and using an empty-denominator-window argument to control intermediate cusp excursions without further pruning the tree.

Larger sieve with height function and uniform bounds for integral points on curves over number fields

Saunak Bhattacharjee

If a set of algebraic integers is 'sparse' modulo every prime ideal, how large can it be? Using a refined version of the 'larger sieve'—a number-theoretic tool bounding sets with density restrictions modulo primes—this paper improves the known bound from H^{2α} to H^α(log H)^r. The improvement yields new uniform bounds on integral points on curves over number fields, a central problem in Diophantine geometry.

math.NT
Original abstract

Let $A \subseteq \mathcal{O}_{K}$ be a set of algebraic integers of height up to $H$ such that $|A \mod{\mathfrak{p}}|\leq α|\mathcal{O}_{K}/\mathfrak{p}|$ for every prime ideal $\mathfrak{p}$ with $N\mathfrak{p}>c$ for some $α\in (0,1)$. It follows from a larger sieve due to Ellenberg, Elsholtz, Hall and Kowalski that $|A| \ll_{K,c,α}H^{2α}$. In this paper, we improve on this larger sieve bound by showing that $|A|\ll_{K,c,α}H^α(\log H)^r$. We also obtain a two-dimensional larger sieve of Helfgott and Venkatesh type over $\mathcal{O}_{K} \times \mathcal{O}_{K}$ and apply it to produce a Bombieri-Pila type bound over $\mathcal{O}_{K}$.

Algebraic values of transcendental power series with geometric coefficient moduli

Diego Marques

Can a power series be transcendental as a function yet consistently produce algebraic outputs at algebraic inputs? This paper constructs uncountably many such series whose coefficients all have moduli equal to powers of a fixed algebraic number, proving all derivatives also take algebraic values at algebraic points despite the series being transcendental over ℂ(z). This sharpens our understanding of how algebraic and transcendental behavior can coexist in analytic functions.

math.NT
Original abstract

Let $λ>1$ be a real algebraic number. We construct continuum many power series $f(z)=\sum_{k\geq0}a_kz^k$ of radius of convergence exactly one such that every nonzero coefficient $a_k$ is algebraic and has modulus $λ^m$ for some $m\geq0$. Moreover, for every integer $s\geq0$, the derivative $f^{(s)}$ takes algebraic values at all algebraic points of the open unit disk and is transcendental over $\mathbb{C}(z)$. The proof combines algebraic polygonal cancellation with a sparse polynomial-block argument. This shows that a multiplicative rank-one restriction on coefficient moduli is compatible with algebraicity of the full analytic jet at every algebraic point once algebraic phases are allowed.

Self-Referential Leading Digits of Exponential Sequences: Arithmetic Structure and Certified Search

Zihang Fang

For a base b and constant c > 1, when does c^m start with the digits of m itself? This paper gives an exact 'shrinking target' criterion for these self-referential leading digits, proves infinitely many such m exist under mild conditions, and establishes an eventual two-gap law for candidate values using Lambert W-function inversion. The result provides certified algorithms and connects the problem to discrepancy theory and Benford's law.

math.NT
Original abstract

For $c>1$ and an integer radix $b\ge2$, we study the positive integers $m$ for which $mb^k\le c^m<(m+1)b^k$ for some $k\ge0$; for integer $c$, this is the self-prefix leading-digit condition. We derive an exact shrinking-target criterion; for $c\ge2$, an exact signed-discrepancy identity isolates both infinitude and the conjectural logarithmic count. For $c\ge2$ with nonintegral logarithmic slope, Lambert $W_{-1}$ inversion produces a candidate sequence with an eventual two-gap law and an exact counting formula; for $(c,b)=(2,10)$ all consecutive candidate gaps are $3$ or $4$. For algebraic $c$ with irrational $\log_b c$, the Lambert-root phases satisfy deterministic moving-target asymptotics in an explicit nontrivial power range strictly below the critical scale. For irrational logarithmic slope, actual hits obey fixed-difference and arithmetic-chain rigidity; for multiplicatively independent integer parameters, coherent endpoint hits at floor resonance centers force every intermediate term. Finally, set $ρ=\{\log_b c\}$. For fixed multiplicatively independent integers $c,b$, an interpolated continued-fraction locator has bit complexity $O(N^{1-1/ν}\operatorname{polylog}N)$ for every $ν>μ(ρ)$. We give an explicit certified instance for $(2,10)$, whose infinitude remains open.

Algebraic Geometry

29

Boundedness of polarized foliated surfaces

Yen-An Chen, Minzhe Zhu

Foliated surfaces are algebraic surfaces equipped with a foliation (a decomposition into curves), and 'boundedness' means the objects in question form a finite-parameter family. This paper runs the minimal model program for these objects and proves that well-behaved foliated surfaces with controlled volume lie in a bounded family. This extends classical results from algebraic geometry to the foliated setting, unlocking tools like effective birationality for classifying them.

math.AG
Original abstract

We establish the minimal model program for klt adjoint foliated surfaces and use it to study the boundedness of polarized adjoint foliated surfaces. We prove that $ε$-lc adjoint foliated surfaces with nef adjoint canonical divisor and a nef and big integral polarization form a bounded family, provided that the volume of their sum is bounded from above. As applications, we establish boundedness and effective birationality for adjoint foliated surfaces of general type, as well as a uniform positive lower bound for their volumes. Finally, for $ε$-lc Calabi--Yau adjoint foliated surfaces, we prove that the volumes of the canonical divisors of rank one foliations on the underlying surfaces belong to a fixed discrete set.

Stability conditions and moduli spaces on projective families

Chunyi Li, Zhiyu Liu, Ziqi Liu et al.

Stability conditions are tools in algebraic geometry that let you sort geometric objects (sheaves) into 'semistable' families and build well-behaved parameter spaces called moduli spaces. This paper generalizes that machinery from single spaces to entire families of spaces varying over an arbitrary base, proving the resulting moduli spaces are proper (compact in an algebraic sense) and resolving a conjecture of Halpern-Leistner and Robotis along the way.

math.AG
Original abstract

We extend the construction of stability conditions on projective schemes over a field to projective families over an arbitrary base, and prove that they admit proper relative moduli spaces of semistable objects. We also prove a number of complementary results: the existence of mass-Hom bounds for these stability conditions, as conjectured by Halpern-Leistner and Robotis; a comparison with tilt-stability on surfaces and threefolds; a construction of stability conditions on the supported derived category of total spaces of certain vector bundles, including all local Calabi--Yau varieties; and a simple new proof of Bondal and Orlov's reconstruction theorem.

Families of curves separating points

Wojciech Porowski

A hyperbolic curve is a curve whose universal cover is the upper half-plane—think genus ≥ 2. This paper shows that, given finitely many rational points on such a curve, one can find a finite étale (unramified) cover and a family of curves over it whose fibers above those points have Jacobians—associated abelian varieties—that are pairwise nonisogenous (share no common abelian variety factor). The result has applications to understanding Galois sections in anabelian geometry.

math.AG
Original abstract

We show that for a hyperbolic curve X over an algebraically closed field k of characteristic zero and a finite set S of k-rational points of X, after replacing X by a finite étale cover, there exists a family of curves over X whose fibres over points in S have pairwise nonisogenous Jacobians. We also give an application of our results to a problem concerning Galois sections.

Sharp Bounds for Totally Invariant Cycles of Projective Varieties

Wentao Chang, Yujie Luo

How many cycles can a self-map of a projective variety 'trap' under repeated iteration? For smooth projective varieties with a well-behaved self-map (called an int-amplified endomorphism), this paper proves a sharp upper bound on how many prime cycles can become fixed under some iterate of the map. On projective space P^n, the count of totally invariant cycles of each dimension is bounded by a binomial coefficient, and this bound is tight.

math.AG
Original abstract

Let $X$ be a smooth projective variety, $f:X\to X$ an int-amplified endomorphism, and $L$ any ample line bundle on $X$. We prove that, in every codimension, the total degree of prime cycles that become totally invariant under an iterate of $f$ satisfies an explicit Hilbert-function bound on their total $L$-degree. In particular, when $X=\mathbf{P}^n$, the number of totally invariant prime $(n-r)$-cycle is bounded by $\binom{n+1}{r}$, and this bound is optimal.

On the boundedness of elliptic Calabi-Yau 4-folds

Stefano Filipazzi, Fulin Xu

Calabi-Yau 4-folds—complex geometric spaces important in string theory—come in potentially infinitely many shapes, so a central question is whether they can be organized into finitely many families (boundedness). This paper shows that elliptic Calabi-Yau 4-folds, except for a specific product-type construction, fall into finitely many algebraic families, a major step toward classifying these spaces systematically.

math.AG
Original abstract

In this work, we settle the boundedness of a vast class of elliptic Calabi-Yau 4-folds. In particular, we show that any elliptic Calabi-Yau 4-fold that is not crepant to the quotient of a product $Y \times E$, where $E$ is an elliptic curve and $Y$ a Calabi-Yau 3-fold, belongs to finitely many algebraic families. In particular, the statement applies whenever the elliptic fibration of the Calabi-Yau 4-fold is not isotrivial. We also provide partial evidence for the boundedness of the middle Betti number of Calabi-Yau 3-folds and study the index of fibered $K$-trivial 4-folds.

The finite degree formula for normalized volumes

Zhiyu Liu

Normalized volume is a numerical invariant measuring the complexity of a singularity in algebraic geometry. This paper proves that for a finite map between singularities that preserves the canonical class, the normalized volume simply scales by the map's degree—confirming a conjecture from multiple recent works. This clean multiplicativity result deepens our understanding of how the minimal log discrepancy, a key measure of singularity severity, behaves under finite covers.

math.AG math.AC math.DG
Original abstract

Let $f\colon \big(x\in (X, Δ_X)\big)\to \big(y\in (Y, Δ_Y)\big)$ be a finite surjective morphism between klt singularities such that $K_X+Δ_X=f^*(K_Y+Δ_Y)$. We show that the normalized volumes satisfy \[\widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(x, X, Δ_X)=\mathrm{deg}(f)\cdot \widehat{\mathrm{vol}}(y, Y, Δ_Y).\] This proves a conjecture in [LLX20, Zhu25, XZ26a].

Splitting Brauer classes by genus one curves over number fields

Benjamin Antieau, Asher Auel, Federico Scavia

A Brauer class over a field measures an obstruction to certain algebraic structures; it is 'split' by a field extension if the obstruction vanishes over that extension. This paper proves that every Brauer class over a number field is split by a genus one curve (an elliptic-curve-like object), by showing every associated Severi-Brauer variety contains such a curve. The proof uses the fibration method and Brauer-Manin obstructions to establish density of rational points.

math.AG math.NT
Original abstract

We prove that every Severi-Brauer variety of dimension at least two over a number field contains a twisted elliptic normal curve. Consequently, every Brauer class over a number field is split by a genus one curve. We prove this using the fibration method, by showing that the rational points on a smooth compactification of the Hilbert scheme of twisted elliptic normal curves are dense in its Brauer-Manin set.

A non-holomorphic P=W phenomenon

Zili Zhang

The P=W conjecture connects two different filtrations (perverse filtration on geometry, weight filtration on algebra) arising in mirror symmetry for certain spaces called cluster varieties. This paper establishes P=W for isolated 3-dimensional cluster varieties without a previously required 'full rank' assumption, crucially handling singular varieties whose associated geometric structures are real-analytic rather than complex algebraic—a genuinely new non-holomorphic setting.

math.AG
Original abstract

We prove the P=W identity for isolated cluster varieties of dimension 3 without the full rank hypothesis. These cluster varieties are generally singular, and the associated Lagrangian fibrations are not complex algebraic. On the P side, we construct the perverse truncation by a detailed analysis of the explicit real-analytic geometry of the Lagrangian fibration. On the W side, we construct a natural non-proper algebraic morphism from the cluster varieties and investigate the decomposition of the derived push-forward of the constant sheaf along this morphism within the derived category of mixed Hodge modules. In the P=W phenomenon for non-full rank isolated cluster varieties of dimension 3, both the curious hard Lefschetz property on the W side and the relative hard Lefschetz property on the P side fail.

Fundamental group schemes of globally F-regular varieties

Charles Vial

In positive-characteristic algebraic geometry, globally F-regular varieties are a well-behaved class sharing properties with log terminal singularities in characteristic zero. This paper proves that their Nori fundamental group scheme — an algebraic object encoding covers of the variety — is finite and linearly reductive, meaning every covering is 'tame.' This is a positive-characteristic analogue of Xu and Braun's theorems on finiteness of fundamental groups of klt singularities, resolving a key open problem in the field.

math.AG
Original abstract

We prove that every tower of connected quasi-torsor covers of a connected normal globally $F$-regular projective variety over an algebraically closed field of positive characteristic stabilizes, and that the Nori fundamental group scheme of its regular locus is finite and linearly reductive. The linear reductivity asserts that any quasi-torsor cover is tame, while the finiteness is a positive-characteristic analogue of theorems of Xu and Braun on the finiteness of the fundamental group of the smooth locus of a log Fano complex projective variety.

On the Betti numbers, Poincaré polynomials, and Euler characteristics of $\overline{\mathcal M}_{0,n}$

Giordano Cotti

The moduli space M̄₀,ₙ parametrizes stable configurations of n labeled points on a sphere, and understanding its topology — encoded in Betti numbers and Poincaré polynomials — has attracted sustained interest. This paper gives elementary, unified derivations of two recently discovered closed-form formulas for these polynomials, showing both are secretly encoded in a classical generating function from the 1990s and clarifying their combinatorial relationship.

math.AG math-ph math.CO
Original abstract

In this paper, we revisit the Poincaré polynomials, Betti numbers, and Euler characteristics of the Deligne-Mumford moduli spaces $\overline{\mathcal M}_{0,n}$ of stable $n$-pointed rational curves. We give elementary derivations of two recent closed formulas for their Poincaré polynomials, due respectively to Aluffi-Marcolli-Nascimento (arXiv:2406.13095) and to Eur-Ferroni-Matherne-Pagaria-Vecchi (arXiv:2504.16776). Our approach shows that both formulas are already implicit in the generating-series results of Getzler and Manin, and can be extracted from them by elementary manipulations of generating functions, the binomial series, and standard identities for Stirling numbers. Beyond these new derivations, the same method also yields new linear recurrence relations for refined invariants associated with these Poincaré polynomials, namely distinguished summands and a bivariate refinement. As a further consequence, we obtain two additional formulas for the Betti numbers, not previously recorded in this form. We also study the Euler characteristics $χ(\overline{\mathcal M}_{0,n})$. Using the Taylor expansion of a suitable branch of the Lambert $W$-function, we show that their sequence is obtained by evaluating complete Bell polynomials at an explicit auxiliary integer sequence. This Bell-polynomial representation yields Hessenberg determinantal formulas and a new linear recursion, distinct from the well-known quadratic Keel-Manin recursion. It also provides an explicit extraction of the Euler characteristics from the Lambert $W$-function expression considered by Aluffi-Marcolli-Nascimento. Finally, we refine the Manin-Zagier asymptotic estimate for these Euler characteristics by computing the full asymptotic expansion.

Twelve common flex lines in a general pencil of cubics

Jihao Liu, Yanze Wang

A pencil of cubics is a one-parameter family of degree-3 curves sharing two base points; a flex line is a line meeting a cubic with multiplicity at least 3. This paper proves that a general such pencil over the complex numbers has exactly 12 common flex lines, resolving an open question of Ciliberto, Miranda, and Roé, with the key result discovered using generative AI.

math.AG
Original abstract

We prove that a general pencil of plane cubics over $\mathbb C$ has exactly $12$ common flex lines. This answers a question of Ciliberto, Miranda, and Roé. The main result of this paper was obtained using generative AI, particularly ChatGPT 5.5 Pro and the Danus system.

Higher dimensional dominoes in de Rham-Witt cohomology

Yuanning Zhang

De Rham–Witt cohomology is a tool for studying algebraic varieties in characteristic p, and the domino is the poorly-understood summand that carries nontrivial differentials in its associated spectral sequence. This paper classifies two-dimensional dominoes and puts dominoes of any dimension into a normal form, attaching two natural group-theoretic invariants whose isogeny classes fully distinguish them.

math.AG
Original abstract

The de Rham-Witt cohomology of a smooth proper variety in characteristic $p$ contains a canonical piece called the domino, which is not finitely generated over the Witt vectors and carries the nonzero differentials of the slope spectral sequence. Beyond dimension one, dominoes were unclassified. We classify the two-dimensional ones and put a domino of any dimension into a normal form. To each domino we attach two unipotent groups, one formal and one perfect, and prove that their isogeny partitions agree. In degree two we recover the domino of a Mazur-Ogus variety from its crystalline cohomology, compute it for two families of supersingular abelian varieties, and bound the exponent of the $p$-primary Brauer group in terms of the $a$-number, for every prime $p$. This answers a question of Grammatica-Skorobogatov-Yang.

The Cautis-Logvinenko conjecture

Alastair Craw, Ryo Yamagishi

For a finite subgroup G of SL(3,ℂ), the G-Hilbert scheme is a geometric space resolving the singularities of the quotient ℂ³/G. The Cautis-Logvinenko conjecture predicts that certain algebraic objects (sheaves associated to representations of G) remain 'pure'—without lower-dimensional components—under a key derived equivalence. The authors prove this for all G whose McKay quiver (a graph encoding representation structure) has no loops, settling an important case in the McKay correspondence program.

math.AG math.RT
Original abstract

For a finite subgroup $G\subset \operatorname{SL}(3,\mathbb{C})$, the Cautis--Logvinenko conjecture states that for each nontrivial irreducible representation $ρ$ of $G$, the image of the sheaf $\mathcal{O}_0\otimes ρ$ under the derived equivalence of Bridgeland--King--Reid is a pure sheaf on the $G$-Hilbert scheme. We prove this when the McKay quiver of $G$ contains no loops; this includes many dihedral and trihedral subgroups of $\operatorname{SL}(3,\mathbb{C})$, as well as six of the eight sporadic finite subgroups. In doing so, we compute the relevant sheaf explicitly whenever its support is of dimension one. Our main result implies that a matrix defining the Gale dual of the linearisation map is sign-coherent, thereby allowing us to read off the support and cohomological degree of the pure sheaves directly from the matrix.

A smooth projective counterexample to Bondal-Polishchuk's conjecture

Anya Nordskova

The derived category D^b(X) of a smooth algebraic variety packages its geometry into algebra; 'full exceptional collections' are special generating sets, and the braid group acts by reordering them. This paper gives the first smooth projective variety counterexample to Bondal–Polishchuk's 1993 conjecture that this braid group action is always transitive.

math.AG
Original abstract

For a particular smooth projective (weak Fano) threefold $X$ we show that the braid group action on the set of full exceptional collections in $D^b(X)$ is not transitive. This provides a counterexample to a conjecture of Bondal and Polishchuk from 1993. The conjecture was first disproved by Chang, Haiden, and Schroll, who constructed a family of partially wrapped Fukaya categories for which the transitivity fails. However, no counterexample of the form $D^b(X)$ for $X$ a smooth projective variety was previously known.

Analytic Bertini theorem II --- The local case

Mingchen Xia

The classical Bertini theorem says that a generic hyperplane slice of a smooth algebraic variety is again smooth. This paper proves the local analytic version of that theorem in full generality, confirming a conjecture of Boucksom. The result extends Bertini's theorem into the analytic (rather than algebraic) setting locally, filling a gap relevant to complex geometry and pluripotential theory.

math.AG math.CV math.DG
Original abstract

We prove the local analytic Bertini theorem, confirming a conjecture of Boucksom in full generality.

The Miyaoka-Yau inequality and the delta invariant for Fano varieties

Tomoyuki Hisamoto, Masataka Iwai

The Miyaoka-Yau inequality classically constrains the geometry of smooth algebraic varieties via their Chern classes (numerical invariants encoding curvature). This paper extends it to singular Fano varieties—higher-dimensional generalizations of projective space—using the delta invariant, a stability measure that quantifies how far the variety is from admitting a special Kähler metric. The result matters because it handles K-unstable Fanos, the previously intractable case, and includes an equivariant version for soliton geometries.

math.AG math.CV math.DG
Original abstract

We establish the following Miyaoka-Yau inequality for any $n$-dimensional klt Fano variety $X$, possibly K-unstable, in terms of its delta invariant: $$ \left(2(n+1)\widehat{c}_2(X)-n c_1(X)^2\right)\cdot c_1(X)^{n-2} \ge -n \left(1-\min\{1,δ(X)\}\right)^2 \cdot c_1(X)^n. $$ Furthermore, inspired by recent work of Inoue and Hallam-Lahdili, we formulate and prove an equivariant version of this inequality in the soliton setting, in which the Chern classes and the delta invariant are replaced by the equivariant Chern classes and the weighted delta invariant, respectively. As a consequence, we obtain the equivariant Miyaoka-Yau inequality for every smooth Fano manifold admitting a Kähler-Ricci soliton.

A counterexample to a conjecture of Duncan on versal actions

Federico Scavia

Versality of a group action is a maximality condition saying the action captures all torsors for that group; Duncan conjectured it suffices to check versality on Sylow subgroups (the largest prime-power subgroups). This paper disproves that conjecture with a concrete counterexample: the group (C_7⋊C_3)×C_2 acting on a degree-2 Del Pezzo surface (a specific algebraic surface). The counterexample shows Sylow-local data fundamentally cannot detect this global geometric property.

math.AG
Original abstract

We disprove a conjecture of Duncan asserting that versality of a faithful finite group action can be detected on Sylow subgroups. Our counterexample is an action of $(C_7\rtimes C_3)\times C_2$ on a degree-$2$ Del Pezzo surface.

On the Kähler MMP and the transcendental base-point-free theorem

Christopher Hacon, Lingyao Xie

The Minimal Model Program (MMP) is a framework for classifying algebraic varieties by simplifying them geometrically. This paper extends the MMP to big generalized klt Kähler pairs—geometric objects defined analytically rather than algebraically—and proves Tosatti's conjecture that certain line bundles on these spaces have global sections, a key structural result previously out of reach.

math.AG
Original abstract

In this article, we establish the minimal model program for big gklt Kähler pairs, and in particular we prove Tosatti's transcendental base-point-free conjecture.

Simply branched covers of curves and wild conductor exponents

Harry Spencer

A curve cover's 'wild conductor exponent' measures how wildly a prime p ramifies in an arithmetic curve, and is notoriously hard to compute. Using deformation theory, this paper shows any cover of curves over a p-adic field can be perturbed into a 'simply branched' cover (where branch points have minimal complexity), then uses this to give an explicit formula for the wild conductor exponent in terms of easily readable ramification data.

math.AG math.NT
Original abstract

We use deformation theory to show that a cover of smooth, projective, geometrically connected curves $π\colon C\to D$ over a $p$-adic field can be $p$-adically perturbed to obtain a nearby simply branched cover $π'\colon C'\to D$ and that, if $D=\mathbb{P}^1$ and $π^*\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^1}(1)$ is very ample, then we may take $C'=C$. As an application, we give a formula for the wild conductor exponent of a curve at a prime $p>d$ in terms of the ramification data of any degree $d$ cover $C\to\mathbb{P}^1$.

From Regions to Hodge Structures: The Topological Study of semialgebraic Curves Configurations

Abolfazl Soltanpour

This paper develops a combinatorial framework for arrangements of semialgebraic curves (solution sets of polynomial inequalities) that intersect at ordinary nodes, proving formulas that count regions and generalize the classical deletion–restriction method from hyperplane arrangements. In the algebraic case, they show that avoiding triple intersection points guarantees the cohomology ring has a clean presentation analogous to the Orlik–Solomon algebra of hyperplane arrangements.

math.AG math.AT math.KT
Original abstract

We develop a combinatorial theory for finite arrangements of connected semialgebraic curves with ordinary multiple intersections, governed by a local node contribution $ψ$ that determines global geometric and topological properties. We prove exact region-count formulas, characterize maximal arrangements, and extend the deletion--restriction recurrence to general curve arrangements. In the algebraic setting, we prove that the absence of triple points ($k_x=3$) is a sufficient condition for the OS-type algebra to factor through cohomology; the converse, however, fails already for line arrangements, where the classical Orlik--Solomon relations ensure factorization even in the presence of triple points. For line arrangements we compute the discrepancy between the simplified OS-model and $H^2$, showing it is governed by nodes with $k_x\ge4$ and equals $\sum_{k_x\ge4}\binom{k_x-1}{2}$. The node contribution appears in the mixed Hodge structure via the Euler characteristic and, for line arrangements, equals $\dim \operatorname{Gr}_4^W H^2$. The defect complex for concurrent lines reveals that exactness obstructions require curve-wise incidence data. Finally, we introduce binomial node invariants $\{Ψ_k\}$, prove $Ψ_2$ is the universal linearly locally additive invariant, and show $ψ=Ψ_1-Ψ_0$.

Roots of Bernstein-Sato polynomials for projective hypersurfaces with ordinary double points

Seung-Jo Jung, Morihiko Saito

The Bernstein-Sato polynomial is an algebraic invariant encoding singularity data of a polynomial, with its roots governing nearby cycles and Hodge theory. For projective hypersurfaces with only ordinary double points (the mildest singularities), this paper explicitly computes those roots in terms of degree and singular-point count. The result gives a clean, computable formula connecting global geometry to fine analytic invariants.

math.AG
Original abstract

Let $X\subset{\mathbb P}^{n-1}$ be a hypersurface of degree $d\ge3$ with ordinary double points, where $n\ge3$. The roots of Bernstein-Sato polynomial of its defining polynomial $f$ are given up to sign by 1, $(n-1)/2$, and $j/d$ for $j\in{\mathbb Z}\cap[n,nd-n-p_f]$ with $p_f$ a positive integer. Here $p_f$ is bounded above by the minimal positive integer $q_s$ satisfying $\binom{q_s+n-1}{n-1}>s:=|{\rm Sing}\,X|$, and we can verify that $p_f$ coincides with $q_s$ in the case the singular points of $X$ are in ``general position". We show that this upper bound is sharp in the case $\binom{\lfloor d/2\rfloor+n-2}{n-1}\ge s$ or $\binom{d+n-3}{n-1}\ge sn$ by providing a homogeneous polynomial of degree $d$ such that the associated projective hypersurface has ordinary double points at given $s$ points in sufficiently general position and is nonsingular outside them (using a theorem of Alexander and Hirschowitz for the second case). It is conjectured that the above sharp bound under the first hypothesis can be extended naturally to the case where $X$ has only $A_2$-singularities instead of ordinary double points.

Biangular lines with angles arccos(1/5) and arccos(3/5)

Paul Tricot

Biangular line systems are collections of lines through the origin where all pairwise angles take exactly two values — here arccos(1/5) and arccos(3/5) — and finding the largest such systems is an open combinatorial geometry problem. This paper uses connections to integral lattices (grids of integer-coordinate points) to fully classify the largest such systems in dimensions 7 through 10, and constructs a new record-tying system in dimension 15. These systems matter for coding theory and quantum information.

math.AG
Original abstract

The largest known biangular lines systems in dimension 7 through 20 are due to Ganzhinov and Szöllősi, and the lines make angles $\arccos(\frac{1}{5})$ and $\arccos(\frac{3}{5})$. They are constructed by making use of the nice connection with integral lattices. We explore further the connection between biangular lines and integral lattices to classify the largest biangular line systems with these angles, in dimension 7 through 10. We also give a new biangular line system in dimension 15, that matches the size of the largest known in this dimension.

Stratified motivic invariants and bivariate deformations of Poincaré polynomials

Gergely Bérczi, Young-Hoon Kiem

This paper studies 'motivic invariants' — algebraic tools that capture topological and geometric information about spaces — for stratified varieties, which are spaces built from nested layers. The authors find clean recursive formulas for these invariants on important moduli spaces (parameter spaces for geometric objects). The result connects abstract invariant theory to concrete computations in moduli theory, yielding polynomial invariants that interpolate between a space's boundary and interior.

math.AG
Original abstract

For a stratified variety $X$ and a motivic invariant $\mathbb{H}$, we consider the stratified invariant which interpolates the invariant of $X$ and that of its interior $X^{\circ}$. Based on observations in moduli theory, we introduce the notion of an echelon tower of stratified varieties and then prove explicit inductive formulae for the stratified invariants of the moduli spaces $\overline{M}_{0,n}$ of stable curves of genus $0$ and the Fulton-MacPherson varieties $Y[n]$ for any smooth projective variety $Y$. Using these, we show that the mysterious bivariate deformations of the even degree Poincaré polynomials of $\overline{M}_{0,n+1}$ and $\mathbb{P}^1[n]$ in \cite{BercziKiem2026} are nothing but stratified virtual Poincaré polynomials.

Projective moduli of log Calabi--Yau fibrations over curves

Giovanni Inchiostro, Junyan Zhao

How do you build a well-behaved parameter space for certain geometric objects called log Calabi–Yau fibrations (spaces that fiber over curves with special curvature properties)? The authors introduce a new stability condition—a rule for deciding which objects to include—that produces a proper Deligne–Mumford stack with a projective coarse moduli space, meaning a rigorous, geometrically nice 'catalog' of such objects. The boundary of this catalog still captures degenerate cases, making it useful for compactification problems.

math.AG
Original abstract

We introduce a new stability condition for log Calabi--Yau fibrations over curves. We prove that it gives rise to a proper Deligne--Mumford stack with a projective coarse moduli space, whose boundary still parametrizes flat fibrations over curves.

The Chow Characteristic Image of \(\Spin(10)\) via the Affine Cone over the Spinor Variety

Sanghoon Baek

For the spin group Spin(10), mathematicians want to know which algebraic cycles—tracked by the Chow ring of its classifying space—survive after restricting to a maximal torus. This paper pins down exactly that image, resolving a gap in our understanding of the Chow characteristic map for exceptional cases. The key tool is a clever geometric construction using the spinor variety (a space encoding the group's spin representation), showing how sophisticated algebraic geometry can crack stubborn computational problems in algebraic K-theory.

math.AG
Original abstract

Let \(G=\Spin(10)\) be the split spin group over a field of characteristic different from \(2\), and let \(T\subset G\) be a split maximal torus. We determine the image of the integral Chow restriction map \(\CH(BG)\to \CH(BT)^W\), equivalently \(\CH(BG)\) modulo torsion. The main new geometric ingredient in the proof is a construction of the class \(c_2c_3c_5\), where the \(c_i\) are the elementary Chern classes after restriction to \(T\). This class is obtained from the proper equivariant push-forward associated with the affine cone over the spinor variety in its half-spin embedding for the special Clifford group \(Γ^+(10)\). Modulo two, the image is the subring generated, over the smallest Steenrod-stable subring of \(\F[c_2,c_3,c_4,c_5]\) containing \(c_2^2,c_3^2,c_4^2,c_5\) and \(c_2c_3c_5\), by the torus restriction of the top Chern class of a half-spin representation. The integral characteristic image is the full inverse image of this mod-two subring under reduction modulo \(2\).

Engineered Complete Intersections: Algorithmic Aspects

Alexander Esterov, Rafael Mohr, Yulia Mukhina

Engineered Complete Intersections are sparse polynomial systems appearing in enumerative geometry and chemical network modeling. This paper develops algorithms to analyze their solutions, generalizing a classical geometric tool called mixed subdivisions to count and locate roots more effectively. The work bridges theoretical combinatorics with practical computation for systems that standard tools handle poorly due to sparsity.

cs.SC math.AG
Original abstract

Engineered Complete Intersections (ECI's) are a class of sparse polynomial systems frequently arising in a number of contexts, both in pure mathematics (e.g. enumerative geometry) and applications (e.g. chemical reaction networks). Based on theoretical results given by the first author, we give several contributions. First we give a new effective technique to tropicalize such systems by generalizing the classical notion of mixed subdivisions introduced by Huber and Sturmfels (1995) to ECI's with the particular goal to efficiently count solutions of square systems of equations in ECI form. We further design a tropical homotopy continuation algorithm for computing such mixed subdivisions, inspired by Jensen (2016), Malajovich (2017) and Daisey and Ren (2024). Our techniques can be used to numerically solve such systems by coupling them with the algorithms introduced by Helminck, Henriksson and Ren (2024). Finally, we give an algorithm to compute Newton polytopes of eliminants of ECI's. This gives a new way to compute, for example, Newton polytopes of so-called $A$-discriminants. Coupled with evaluation-interpolation paradigms our algorithm gives an efficient approach to compute such eliminants. We implemented our algorithms in the form of a software package which we use to demonstrate their practical feasibility on a range of examples.

Actions of $(\mathbb{Z}/4)^4$ on rationally connected threefolds

Konstantin Loginov

The Cremona group Cr₃(ℂ) consists of all birational self-maps of three-dimensional complex space, and a central question is which finite groups embed in it. This paper proves that (ℤ/4)⁴—a specific 256-element abelian group—acts on a rationally connected threefold (a well-connected algebraic variety) but cannot embed in Cr₃(ℂ), completing the full classification of which groups of this form do or don't embed.

math.AG
Original abstract

Let $G=(\mathbb{Z}/4)^4$. We prove that if $X$ is a rationally connected threefold with a faithful action of $G$, then $X$ is $G$-birational to the Fermat quartic threefold. If $X$ is a terminal $G\mathbb{Q}$-Fano threefold, this birational equivalence is biregular. Consequently, the group $G$ acts faithfully on a rationally connected threefold but does not embed into $\operatorname{Cr}_3(\mathbb{C})$. Combined with earlier results, this yields a complete classification of the pairs $(m,r)$ for which $(\mathbb{Z}/m)^r$ embeds into $\operatorname{Cr}_3(\mathbb{C})$, and of those for which it embeds into $\operatorname{Bir}(X)$ for a rationally connected threefold $X$.

Sylow Criteria for Liftability of Automorphism Groups of Smooth Hypersurfaces

Baiting Xie, Zhiwei Zheng

When can a group of symmetries of a geometric object be 'lifted' to a richer algebraic setting? This paper gives a clean Sylow criterion: a finite symmetry group of a hypersurface is liftable if and only if certain prime-power subgroups (Sylow subgroups) are liftable, reducing a global problem to local ones. This dramatically simplifies checking liftability by focusing on one prime at a time.

math.AG
Original abstract

In this paper, we first establish Sylow criteria for liftability of finite subgroups of the projective linear groups over arbitrary field. We also obtain Sylow criteria for $F$-liftability: if $F$ is a nonsingular polynomial of degree $d$ in $N$ variables over a field of characteristic zero, then a finite subgroup $G$ of $\mathrm{Lin}(F)$ is $F$-liftable if and only if for every prime $p$ dividing $\gcd(|G|,N,d)$, there exists a Sylow $p$-subgroup of $G$ that is $F$-liftable. Our proof combines restriction and corestriction in group cohomology with the reduction-to-Klein method.

Unramified Motivic Multiple Mixed Values

Ce Xu, Jianqiang Zhao

Multiple zeta values are a family of real numbers built from nested infinite sums, and multiple mixed values are variants where summation indices obey parity constraints. A central question is which mixed values collapse back to ordinary multiple zeta values. This paper makes progress at the motivic level—a formal algebraic proxy for the actual numbers—by characterizing an 'unramified' subclass that provably lives in the simpler level-one world.

math.AG
Original abstract

The multiple mixed values (MMVs) are level two variants of multiple zeta values produced by restricting the summation indices to fixed parity patterns. One particularly interesting problem is to determine exactly when such values are actually in level one, namely, expressible in terms of multiple zeta values. To solve this completely is beyond our current knowledge since it calls for new ideas from transcendental number theory. However, much progress has been made on the motivic level. Previously, using the descent theory developed by Brown et al. we have tackled this problem for a few special classes of MMVs with regular parity patterns among the summation indices, including Hoffman's multiple $t$-values, Kaneko and Tsumura's multiple $T$-values, and our own multiple $S$-values. In this paper, we turn to the general case and determine completely all the unramified motivic MMVs of depth less than four as well as a few families of unbounded depths. At the end of the paper, we will present some general conjectures to describe the unramified MMVs at all depths greater than three.

Analysis of PDEs

43

On Sirakov's equal-frequency uniqueness conjecture

Hong-Ge Chen, Yong Liu, Juncheng Wei et al.

This paper resolves Sirakov's conjecture about a coupled nonlinear Schrödinger system in 2D and 3D: when both equations share the same frequency, the only positive solution (up to spatial translation) is the 'synchronized state,' where the two components are proportional. Uniqueness results for coupled PDE systems are rare and technically hard; this confirms a clean classification in a physically relevant model describing interacting wave packets.

math.AP math-ph
Original abstract

Let $N\in\{2,3\}$, $0<μ_1\leqμ_2$, and $0<β<μ_1$. We prove that the equal-frequency two-component cubic Schrödinger system \[ -Δu+u=μ_1u^3+βuv^2, \qquad -Δv+v=μ_2v^3+βu^2v \quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^N \] has exactly one positive solution in $H^1(\mathbb{R}^N)\times H^1(\mathbb{R}^N)$ modulo simultaneous translations. More precisely, every positive solution is a simultaneous translate of the synchronized state constructed from the unique positive radial solution of $-Δw+w=w^3$ in $\mathbb{R}^N$. This settles Sirakov's equal-frequency uniqueness conjecture throughout the weak-coupling range. The main difficulty in the proof is to exclude radial solutions for which the ratio of the normalized components is nonconstant. After normalization, the two components satisfy scalar equations with a common potential. We construct a weighted Pohozaev functional for the system together with a correction term and prove that both the corrected functional and the associated weighted functional are strictly positive. Combining these sign properties with a radial flux identity and an auxiliary quotient associated with the component ratio forces synchronization.

Finite-time blow-up for the mass-critical half-wave equation with negative energy

Jeongheon Park

The mass-critical half-wave equation models wave propagation with a nonlocal dispersion operator |D| instead of the usual Laplacian. This paper proves the first finite-time blow-up result for negative-energy solutions near the ground-state mass threshold, and gives a precise blow-up rate for the H^{1/2} Sobolev norm. Understanding blow-up rates matters because they reveal the geometry of singularity formation in nonlinear dispersive equations.

math.AP
Original abstract

We study the one-dimensional focusing mass-critical half-wave equation \[ i\partial_tu=|D|u-|u|^2u. \] For even initial data with negative energy and mass slightly above the ground-state mass, we prove finite-time blow-up and obtain the upper bound \[ \|u(t)\|_{\dot H^{1/2}} \lesssim \frac{|\log(T-t)|^{1/4}}{\sqrt{T-t}} \qquad\text{as }t\uparrow T. \] This is the first finite-time blow-up result for negative-energy solutions to the mass-critical half-wave equation in the near-ground-state regime. The proof uses the modulation analysis developed by Merle--Raphaël \cite{MerleRaphael2005AnnMath}. For the half-wave equation, the key missing ingredient is a coercivity estimate for a nonlocal quadratic form generated by the scaling direction. Unlike the local NLS, the half-wave equation does not admit the ODE methods used to establish the corresponding coercivity estimate. Instead, we prove the coercivity estimate by an analytic reduction followed by a rigorous computer-assisted proof. The spectral analysis part reduces the coercivity problem to a finite collection of spectral inequalities by combining constrained Morse index arguments with the Birman--Schwinger principle. The computer-assisted part certifies these inequalities by interval arithmetic using a validated approximation of the ground state obtained by compactifying the real line. Together with the modulation analysis, this coercivity theorem gives the finite-time blow-up result.

A log-log upper bound on blow-up rates for the mass-critical half-wave equation

Taegyu Kim, Soonsik Kwon, Jeongheon Park

When solutions to certain wave equations 'blow up' (become infinite in finite time), how fast does this happen? For the mass-critical half-wave equation — a nonlinear dispersive equation involving a fractional derivative — this paper proves a precise 'log-log' upper bound on the blow-up rate for a natural class of initial conditions, matching bounds known for related equations like the nonlinear Schrödinger equation. This confirms the half-wave equation fits into the same universality class of blow-up behavior.

math.AP
Original abstract

We study finite-time blow-up for the one-dimensional focusing mass-critical half-wave equation \begin{equation*} i\partial_tu=|D|u-|u|^2u. \end{equation*} For even initial data with negative energy and mass slightly above the ground-state mass, we prove the log-log upper bound \begin{equation*} \|u(t)\|_{\dot H^{1/2}}\lesssim \left(\frac{\log|\log(T-t)|}{T-t}\right)^{1/2} \quad \text{as}\quad t\uparrow T. \end{equation*} This gives, for the half-wave equation, the same log-log law upper bound as in the mass-critical nonlinear Schrödinger equation. The proof follows a similar strategy developed by Merle and Raphaël, but requires a new construction of the blow-up profile. Main difficulty arises from the nonlocal operator $|D|$ and the absence of pseudo-conformal symmetry. We construct an almost self-similar profile with exponentially small error by combining tail computations carried out to arbitrary order, depending on a dynamical parameter, with Borel integral summation in $Λ$-analytic spaces. Then, in the modulation analysis, we use a local-virial spectral property proved in the companion paper \cite{Park2026arXiv}.

Osgood meets Ambrosio-DiPerna-Lions

Guido De Philippis, Leonardo Franchi

The Ambrosio-DiPerna-Lions theory guarantees uniqueness of solutions to transport equations when the velocity field has Sobolev regularity, but what if the field is merely Osgood — a weaker, integral-based continuity condition? This paper extends uniqueness to that setting using a novel conserved weighted energy built from Littlewood-Paley decomposition, bypassing the classical commutator estimates. This broadens the class of irregular flows where transport equations remain well-posed.

math.AP
Original abstract

In this note we extend the Ambrosio-DiPerna-Lions theory on the well-posedness of the transport equation to the case in which the vector field satisfies an $L^p$ Osgood condition. In particular we show that bounded distributional solutions to the transport equation are unique and thus renormalized. As opposed to the classical proof, we do not rely on the vanishing of a commutator, but we show that a weighted energy built out of the Littlewood-Paley decomposition of the solution is conserved.

Geometry of strong forces in continuum mechanics

Theodore D. Drivas, Daniil Glukhovskiy

When a particle moves in a steep potential energy well that is minimal along a submanifold M, does it approximately follow constrained motion along M? This paper rigorously establishes that answer is yes, proving convergence to geodesic-like constrained dynamics as the potential becomes infinitely steep. The result gives a precise geometric and analytic foundation for the classical intuition behind constraint forces in mechanics.

math.AP math-ph math.CA physics.flu-dyn
Original abstract

Consider a material point in finite dimensions moving under the influence of a potential force according to Newton's laws. Suppose the potential energy function is a generalized well, strictly convex transverse to a smooth submanifold $M$ on which it is minimal. If the potential is steep, one expects the particle will oscillate rapidly about this submanifold and, if the initial displacement is not too great, should approximately move along $M$ as if it were ideally constrained (e.g. geodesic). This expectation is true if the initial conditions are very well prepared but may fail otherwise - additional potential forces determined by how the Hessian of the potential varies along $M$ may be present. The origin of this force is that the transversal motion acts as a simple harmonic oscillator with a slowly varying frequency, which approximately conserves action, not energy. In this work, we regard continuum mechanical systems such as the elastic thread or compressible fluid as material points moving in an infinite dimensional space according to Newton's laws for appropriate potential energy functionals. We show how to arrive at ideally constrained systems such as the inextensible thread and incompressible fluid as a limit of a strong potential force, computing also corrections to the naive predictions when the data is not very well prepared. For example, for the thread we find a resistance to bending emerge from a strong resistance to compression/expansion. For the fluid, the effective incompressible dynamics may be driven by a remnant acoustical wavefield. Both of these emergent features are nonlinear and non-local. Finally, we give examples of some limits for which the naive models robustly hold because the additional force is trivial. These include the homogeneous incompressible Euler, anelastic Euler, as well as the lake and great lake equations.

Existence of a stable shrinker for the corotational harmonic map heat flow in higher space dimensions

Johannes Angerer, Sarah Kistner, Birgit Schörkhuber

This paper studies how smooth maps from space into a sphere can develop singularities in finite time under heat flow—a gradient-descent process smoothing the map. The authors prove, in dimensions 4–6, the existence of a stable self-similar blowup solution (a 'shrinker'), meaning singularities of this type are robust and not just isolated accidents. This extends prior 3D results and clarifies which finite-time singularities are physically meaningful.

math.AP
Original abstract

We study singularity formation for the heat flow of harmonic maps from $\R^d$ into $\mathbb{S}^d$ in supercritical dimensions $d \in \{3,4,5,6\}$. It is well known that in each of these dimensions there exist infinitely many self-similar solutions that provide examples of loss of regularity in finite time. In this paper, we extend the results of \cite{BieDon18}, \cite{BieDonSch17} for $d=3$ to higher space dimensions $d \in \{4,5,6\}$ and prove the existence of a monotonically increasing self-similar profile $f_0$, which is asymptotically stable under small corotational perturbations. To construct the solution and resolve the spectral problem, we use rigorous computer assistance. As a byproduct of our stability analysis, we also obtain finite-codimension stability of arbitrary self-similar profiles within the corotational class.

Failure of the Weak Sard property without Anomalous Dissipation

Umberto Pappalettera

The 'weak Sard property' controls how solutions to advection-diffusion equations behave; it was conjectured to be necessary to prevent anomalous dissipation—unexpected energy loss in the zero-viscosity limit. This paper disproves that conjecture by constructing an explicit vector field that lacks the weak Sard property yet causes no anomalous dissipation, showing the two phenomena are independent.

math.AP
Original abstract

For every $α\in(0,1)$ we construct an autonomous, divergence-free vector field $u\in C^α_c(\R^2,\R^2)$ that does not have the weak Sard property and, nonetheless, does not induce anomalous dissipation of $L^2(\R^2)$ norm for solutions to the associated advection-diffusion equation. This disproves a conjecture proposed by Bagnara, Boutros, De Lellis and Mayboroda in \cite{BaBoDeMa26}.

Lipschitz continuity of harmonic maps between ${\rm RCD}(K,N)$ spaces and ${\rm CAT}(κ)$ spaces

Luca Gennaioli

Can maps between two different types of curved, possibly fractal-like spaces be smooth? This paper proves that energy-minimizing harmonic maps (functions minimizing a natural energy integral) from RCD(K,N) spaces (metric spaces with synthetic Ricci curvature bounds) into CAT(κ) spaces (spaces with curvature bounded above) are locally Lipschitz continuous—meaning distances can't stretch too wildly. This completes the regularity theory for harmonic maps in the singular setting and validates a key inequality from classical geometry.

math.AP
Original abstract

We are going to prove that energy minimizing harmonic maps from a domain $Ω$ inside an ${\rm RCD}(K,N)$ whose image lies in a small ball inside a ${\rm CAT}(κ)$ space are locally Lipschitz continuous. This completes the picture concerning regularity of harmonic maps between singular spaces and justifies a variant of the Bochner-Eells-Sampson inequality between singular spaces.

Contact set of solutions to gradient flow of Landau-de Gennes energy with a singular entropy potential

Yuning Liu, Xiang Xu

The Landau–de Gennes model describes liquid crystals using a tensor field Q; a singular entropy potential blows up when Q reaches a physical boundary, and the contact set is where this blowup occurs. This paper shows the contact set is empty in dimensions up to three, meaning solutions never hit the singularity, by combining H¹ regularity of the potential with spherical averaging and capacity theory (a tool measuring how 'thin' sets are).

math.AP
Original abstract

This paper investigates the convexity structure and contact set of the $Q$-tensor flow with anisotropic Landau--de Gennes elastic energy and a singular entropy potential $f(Q)$. The flow naturally exhibits a coercivity property, yielding the $H^1$ regularity of $f(Q)$ for almost every time. Combining this $H^1$ regularity with spherical averaging and capacity theory, the contact set $$\mathcal{C}(Q)\triangleq \{x\mid f(Q(x))=\infty\}$$ is characterized. In particular, it is shown to be empty in two spatial dimensions and to have Hausdorff dimension at most one in three spatial dimensions.

Sharp rigidity for quasilinear Liouville equation on manifolds with nonnegative Ricci curvature

Xiaohan Cai

This paper studies the n-Laplacian Liouville equation on curved spaces, asking: when does a solution force the underlying space to be flat Euclidean space? The answer is sharp—if a solution decays at exactly the optimal logarithmic rate, the manifold must be Euclidean and the solution must be a 'standard bubble' (an explicit model solution). This rigidity result is optimal in both the leading coefficient and error term, resolving a geometric PDE question about how analytic behavior pins down geometry.

math.AP math.DG
Original abstract
